I just feel like the part of me that used to reach out for people has gone very quiet. \n\nI used to take a lot of pleasure in my work, and in things like yoga, visiting galleries, or even just journaling. But lately, that's all just faded. I'm mostly just moving through my daily obligations now, without really getting anything out of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a similar period like this before?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I've been stressed before, or just tired from work, but... no, I haven't ever had a period quite like this. It's never just faded out completely like it has now. This is new for me.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's been about eight months. This whole change in... well, how I'm thinking, how I'm working, and just connecting with people... it's all been going on since we took my older relative into our apartment.\n\nDoctor: Has your mood been depressed or down for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel depressed. I know it probably sounds a bit like it, but I don't actually feel sad or down during the day. It's not a heavy or tearful feeling like that... it's really just that the interest and connection are gone. So, no, I wouldn't describe my mood as down.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time when you felt unusually energized or overly upbeat—more than your usual self?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean... I'm generally a pretty even-keeled person. I've never really had a time where I felt unusually upbeat or... you know, had some big burst of energy. If anything, my energy has always just been normal, until this recent stretch where things just feel so flat. So, no, I haven't experienced anything like what you're describing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed or down most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I think you might have just asked me that a moment ago... but no, like I said, I haven't been feeling depressed or sad. It's really just that flat feeling I mentioned, where the interest is gone, but I wouldn't call my mood down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been sleeping differently than usual?\n\nPatient: No, my sleep has been pretty normal. I haven't noticed any changes... I still go to bed and wake up around the same times I usually do. I'm getting about the same amount of sleep as always. So, no, nothing different there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually low in energy or fatigued?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel fatigued. I mean, my energy levels are pretty much what they've always been. I can still do the things I need to do during the day... it's just that I don't really care about doing them anymore. But physically, no, I'm not unusually tired or low on energy.\n\nDoctor: How much has this been affecting your ability to function at work or in your daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: It is definitely affecting things. I mean, I’m still showing up to work every day, but... I’ve missed some grant deadlines lately. And my supervisor has noticed that I'm not functioning at my usual level. She’s actually quietly moved a few of my more demanding clients over to other clinicians. This whole change in... well, how I'm thinking, how I'm working, and just connecting with people... it's all been going on since we took my older relative into our apartment.\n\nDoctor: Has your mood been depressed or down for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel depressed. I know it probably sounds a bit like it, but I don't actually feel sad or down during the day. It's not a heavy or tearful feeling like that... it's really just that the interest and connection are gone. So, no, I wouldn't describe my mood as down.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time when you felt unusually energized or overly upbeat—more than your usual self?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean... I'm generally a pretty even-keeled person. I've never really had a time where I felt unusually upbeat or... you know, had some big burst of energy. So, no, I wouldn't describe my mood as down.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time when you felt unusually energized or overly upbeat—more than your usual self?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean... I'm generally a pretty even-keeled person. I've never really had a time where I felt unusually upbeat or... you know, had some big burst of energy. If anything, my energy has always just been normal, until this recent stretch where things just feel so flat. So, no, I haven't experienced anything like what you're describing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed or down most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I think you might have just asked me that a moment ago... but no, like I said, I haven't been feeling depressed or sad. It's really just that flat feeling I mentioned, where the interest is gone, but I wouldn't call my mood down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been sleeping differently than usual?\n\nPatient: No, my sleep has been pretty normal. I haven't noticed any changes... I still go to bed and wake up around the same times I usually do. I'm getting about the same amount of sleep as always. So, no, nothing different there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually low in energy or fatigued?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel fatigued. I mean, my energy levels are pretty much what they've always been. I can still do the things I need to do during the day... it's just that I don't really care about doing them anymore. But physically, no, I'm not unusually tired or low on energy.\n\nDoctor: How much has this been affecting your ability to function at work or in your daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: It is definitely affecting things. I mean, I’m still showing up to work every day, but... I’ve missed some grant deadlines lately. And my supervisor has noticed that I'm not functioning at my usual level. She’s actually quietly moved a few of my more demanding clients over to other clinicians. So, I'm there, but I'm just not keeping up the way I normally do.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this way on and off for most of your life, or is this only a recent change?\n\nPatient: No, it's definitely a recent change. It hasn't been like this my whole life. This whole shift... you know, the changes in how I'm thinking, my struggles at work, and just not really connecting with people... it's been going on for about eight months now. It started right around the time we moved my older relative into our apartment to live with us.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I wouldn't say I feel sad or depressed. It's more just... flat, I guess? Like I said, I just don't care about things the way I used to, but it's not a sad feeling. I'm not crying or feeling down all day. I just feel kind of empty or detached from it all.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling especially hard on yourself or like you’re a failure more than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I don't feel like a failure or anything. I just... I don't really have the energy to beat myself up over things right now. It's just that lack of caring I mentioned. I'm not really hard on myself, I just kind of exist.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that life isn’t worth living or that you’d be better off dead?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any thoughts like that. I don't feel like life isn't worth living, and I definitely don't think I'd be better off dead. Like I said, it's more of a... a blankness. But I'm not suicidal at all. I don't want to harm myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely. Honestly, that's what finally brought me in here. I just feel like the part of me that used to reach out for people, or take pleasure in things... it’s just gone very quiet. I used to really enjoy my work, doing yoga, visiting art galleries, even just journaling at home. But now, I don't really feel anything toward them. It's not a sad or down feeling, just that flatness I mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two years, have you felt depressed or down for most of the day, on more days than not?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Like I said, it hasn't been a depressed or down feeling. It's just been this... this emptiness for the last eight months or so. Before that, I was fine.
